Schools named after Serra include <a href="/wiki/Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra_High_School_(San_Diego,_California)" class="mw-redirect" title="Junípero Serra High School (San Diego, California)">Junípero Serra High School</a>, a public school in the San Diego community of Tierrasanta, and four Catholic high schools: <a href="/wiki/Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra_High_School_(Gardena,_California)" title="Junípero Serra High School (Gardena, California)">Junípero Serra High School</a> in <a href="/wiki/Gardena,_California" title="Gardena, California">Gardena</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra_High_School_(San_Mateo,_California)" title="Junípero Serra High School (San Mateo, California)">Junipero Serra High School</a> in <a href="/wiki/San_Mateo,_California" title="San Mateo, California">San Mateo</a>, <a href="/wiki/JSerra_Catholic_High_School" title="JSerra Catholic High School">JSerra Catholic High School</a> in <a href="/wiki/San_Juan_Capistrano,_California" title="San Juan Capistrano, California">San Juan Capistrano</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Serra_Catholic_High_School" title="Serra Catholic High School">Serra Catholic High School</a> in <a href="/wiki/McKeesport,_Pennsylvania" title="McKeesport, Pennsylvania">McKeesport, Pennsylvania</a>. There are public elementary schools in <a href="/wiki/San_Francisco" title="San Francisco">San Francisco</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ventura,_California" title="Ventura, California">Ventura</a>, as well as a K-8 Catholic school in <a href="/wiki/Rancho_Santa_Margarita,_California" title="Rancho Santa Margarita, California">Rancho Santa Margarita</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Both Spain and the United States have honored Serra with postage stamps.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1884, the Legislature of California passed a concurrent resolution making August 29 of that year, the centennial of Serra's burial, a legal holiday.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/w/index.php?title=Serra_International&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Serra International (page does not exist)">Serra International</a>, a global lay organization that promotes religious vocations to the Catholic Church, was named in his honor. The group, founded in 1935, currently numbers a membership of about 20,000 worldwide. It also boasts over 1,000 chapters in 44 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Father_Juniperro_Serra_Catholic_Church,_Camarillo.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/Father_Juniperro_Serra_Catholic_Church%2C_Camarillo.JPG/220px-Father_Juniperro_Serra_Catholic_Church%2C_Camarillo.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="150" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/Father_Juniperro_Serra_Catholic_Church%2C_Camarillo.JPG/330px-Father_Juniperro_Serra_Catholic_Church%2C_Camarillo.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/Father_Juniperro_Serra_Catholic_Church%2C_Camarillo.JPG/440px-Father_Juniperro_Serra_Catholic_Church%2C_Camarillo.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3264" data-file-height="2222" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Father_Juniperro_Serra_Catholic_Church,_Camarillo.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Padre_Serra_Parish" title="Padre Serra Parish">St. Junípero Church</a> in <a href="/wiki/Camarillo,_California" title="Camarillo, California">Camarillo</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>Serra's legacy towards <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas" title="Indigenous peoples of the Americas">Native Americans</a> has been a topic of discussion in the Los Angeles area in recent years. The <a href="/wiki/Mexica_Movement" title="Mexica Movement">Mexica Movement</a>, an indigenous separatist group that rejects European influence in the Americas,<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> protested Serra's canonization at the <a href="/wiki/Los_Angeles_Cathedral" class="mw-redirect" title="Los Angeles Cathedral">Los Angeles Cathedral</a> in February 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Huntington_Library" title="Huntington Library">Huntington Library</a> announcement of its 2013 exhibition on Serra made it clear that Serra's treatment of Native Americans would be part of the comprehensive coverage of his legacy.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On September 27, 2015, in response to Serra's canonization, the San Carlos Borromeo de Carmelo Mission was vandalized. The statue of Serra was toppled and splattered with paint, and the cemetery, the mission doors, a fountain, and a crucifix were as well. The message "Saint of Genocide" was put on Serra's tomb, and similar messages were painted elsewhere in the mission courtyard.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> After the incident, law enforcement authorities launched a <a href="/wiki/Hate_crime" title="Hate crime">hate crime</a> investigation since the only grave sites targeted for desecration were those of Europeans.<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2018, Spanish producer <a href="/wiki/Pedro_Alonso_Pablos" title="Pedro Alonso Pablos">Pedro Alonso Pablos</a> made an animation, medium-length film dedicated to the life and work of Fray Junípero called <i>The call of Junipero</i>, and although the Catholic Church had no formal role during the process of creating the film, the vision that the film offers coincides with that of the Church.<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2019, <a href="/wiki/Stanford_University" title="Stanford University">Stanford University</a> renamed two buildings that had formerly been named after Serra: Serra House, where the <a href="/wiki/Clayman_Institute_for_Gender_Research" class="mw-redirect" title="Clayman Institute for Gender Research">Clayman Institute for Gender Research</a> is located was renamed the <a href="/wiki/Carolyn_Attneave" title="Carolyn Attneave">Carolyn Lewis Attneave House</a>, and a student dormitory located in the Lucie Stern Hall complex was renamed the <a href="/wiki/Sally_Ride" title="Sally Ride">Sally Ride</a> House.<sup id="cite_ref-mercurynews1_161-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mercurynews1-161">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> The university followed the recommendations of a committee headed by <a href="/wiki/Paul_Brest" title="Paul Brest">Paul Brest</a> (former dean of Stanford Law School), which had concluded that
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Because the mission system's violence against California Native Americans is part of the history and memory of current members of the community, we believe that features named for Junipero Serra, who was the architect and leader of the mission system, are in tension with (Stanford's) goal of full inclusion.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8201;<cite><sup id="cite_ref-mercurynews1_161-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mercurynews1-161">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup></cite></div></blockquote>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Statuary_and_monuments">Statuary and monuments</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_monuments_and_memorials_removed_during_the_George_Floyd_protests#Junípero_Serra" title="List of monuments and memorials removed during the George Floyd protests">List of monuments and memorials removed during the George Floyd protests §&#160;Junípero Serra</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mission_Dolores-29_(cropped).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Mission_Dolores-29_%28cropped%29.jpg/170px-Mission_Dolores-29_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="183" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Mission_Dolores-29_%28cropped%29.jpg/255px-Mission_Dolores-29_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Mission_Dolores-29_%28cropped%29.jpg/340px-Mission_Dolores-29_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3744" data-file-height="4039" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mission_Dolores-29_(cropped).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Statue at <a href="/wiki/Mission_San_Francisco_de_As%C3%ADs" title="Mission San Francisco de Asís">Mission San Francisco de Asís</a>.</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Fray_Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra,_escultura_de_Alberto_P%C3%A9rez_Soria_(cropped).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/42/Fray_Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra%2C_escultura_de_Alberto_P%C3%A9rez_Soria_%28cropped%29.jpg/170px-Fray_Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra%2C_escultura_de_Alberto_P%C3%A9rez_Soria_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="204" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/42/Fray_Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra%2C_escultura_de_Alberto_P%C3%A9rez_Soria_%28cropped%29.jpg/255px-Fray_Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra%2C_escultura_de_Alberto_P%C3%A9rez_Soria_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/42/Fray_Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra%2C_escultura_de_Alberto_P%C3%A9rez_Soria_%28cropped%29.jpg/340px-Fray_Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra%2C_escultura_de_Alberto_P%C3%A9rez_Soria_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2448" data-file-height="2938" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Fray_Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra,_escultura_de_Alberto_P%C3%A9rez_Soria_(cropped).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Statue in <a href="/wiki/Quer%C3%A9taro_City" title="Querétaro City">Querétaro City</a>.</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:San_Junipero_Serra_(Moraga,_California).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/San_Junipero_Serra_%28Moraga%2C_California%29.jpg/170px-San_Junipero_Serra_%28Moraga%2C_California%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/San_Junipero_Serra_%28Moraga%2C_California%29.jpg/255px-San_Junipero_Serra_%28Moraga%2C_California%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/San_Junipero_Serra_%28Moraga%2C_California%29.jpg/340px-San_Junipero_Serra_%28Moraga%2C_California%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3024" data-file-height="4032" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:San_Junipero_Serra_(Moraga,_California).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Plaque at <a href="/wiki/Saint_Mary%27s_College_of_California" title="Saint Mary&#39;s College of California">St. Mary's College of California</a>.</div></div></div>
<ul><li>A <a href="/wiki/Statue_of_Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra_(U.S._Capitol)" title="Statue of Junípero Serra (U.S. Capitol)">statue of Junípero Serra</a> is one of two statues that represent the state of California in the <a href="/wiki/National_Statuary_Hall_Collection" title="National Statuary Hall Collection">National Statuary Hall Collection</a> in the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Capitol" title="United States Capitol">United States Capitol</a>. The work of <a href="/wiki/Ettore_Cadorin" title="Ettore Cadorin">Ettore Cadorin</a>, it depicts Serra holding a cross and looking skyward. In February 2015, State Senator <a href="/wiki/Ricardo_Lara" title="Ricardo Lara">Ricardo Lara</a> introduced a bill in the California legislature to remove the statue and replace it with one of astronaut <a href="/wiki/Sally_Ride" title="Sally Ride">Sally Ride</a>. In May 2015, some California Catholics were organizing to keep Serra's statue in place. California Governor <a href="/wiki/Jerry_Brown" title="Jerry Brown">Jerry Brown</a> supported retaining it when he visited the Vatican in July 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-siders_163-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-siders-163">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> On July 2, Lara announced that as a gesture of respect towards <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> and people of faith, the vote on the bill would be postponed until the following year. Pope Francis canonized Serra as part of his September 2015 papal visit to the US.<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Statues_of_Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra_(Ventura,_California)" title="Statues of Junípero Serra (Ventura, California)">statue of Serra</a> in <a href="/wiki/Ventura,_California" title="Ventura, California">Ventura, California</a>, standing 9 feet, 4 inches, was displayed in front of <a href="/wiki/Ventura_County_Courthouse" title="Ventura County Courthouse">Ventura City Hall</a> between 1936 to 2020. The original concrete statue was declared Ventura Historic Landmark No. 3 in 1974. A bronze cast replaced the concrete statue in 1989. A wooden replica, created by local carvers, was put on public display in the atrium of Ventura City Hall in 1988. In 2020, city, church, and tribal leaders agreed to move it off public land. Upon city council approval, the bronze cast was placed in storage. Council also voted to remove the wooden replica from public display.<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Douglas_Tilden" title="Douglas Tilden">Douglas Tilden</a> <a href="/wiki/Statue_of_Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra_(San_Francisco)" title="Statue of Junípero Serra (San Francisco)">statue of Serra</a>, representing him as the apostolic preacher in a heroic scale, was toppled on June 19, 2020 at <a href="/wiki/Golden_Gate_Park" title="Golden Gate Park">Golden Gate Park</a> in San Francisco. The tear-down was part of a <a href="/wiki/Juneteenth" title="Juneteenth">Juneteenth</a> protest.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>In 1899, Jane Elizabeth Lathrop Stanford, wife of <a href="/wiki/Leland_Stanford" title="Leland Stanford">Leland Stanford</a>, governor and U.S. Senator from California, and a non-Catholic herself, commissioned a <a href="/wiki/Statue_of_Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra_(Monterey,_California)" title="Statue of Junípero Serra (Monterey, California)">granite monument to Serra</a> which was erected in <a href="/wiki/Monterey,_California" title="Monterey, California">Monterey</a> in 1891. The figure of Serra was decapitated in October 2015, and the head not found until April 2, 2016, in Monterey Bay.<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>When <a href="/wiki/Interstate_280_(California)" title="Interstate 280 (California)">Interstate 280</a> was built in stages from <a href="/wiki/Daly_City,_California" title="Daly City, California">Daly City</a> to <a href="/wiki/San_Jose,_California" title="San Jose, California">San Jose</a> in the 1960s, it was named the <a href="/wiki/Junipero_Serra_Freeway" class="mw-redirect" title="Junipero Serra Freeway">Junipero Serra Freeway</a>. A statue of Serra on a hill on the northbound side of the freeway in <a href="/wiki/Hillsborough,_California" title="Hillsborough, California">Hillsborough, California</a>, points a finger towards the Santa Cruz Mountains and the Pacific.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>  After the freeway's Peninsula segment was finished in the mid-1970s, Caltrans erected small nonstandard wooden signs at each end of the segment (near the interchanges with State Route 1 in Daly City and Foothill Expressway in Cupertino) to proclaim: "The Junipero Serra Freeway [¶] The World's Most Beautiful Freeway." During the 1980s and 1990s, both signs were visible to travelers between San Francisco and San Jose, but were severely damaged by car accidents during the 2000s and were not replaced. The only indicator of the signs' existence is that one of two support posts remains standing at both former locations.</li>
<li>The statue of Serra located at <a href="/wiki/Mission_Santa_Barbara" title="Mission Santa Barbara">Mission Santa Barbara</a> was decapitated in September 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-Scheibe_106-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Scheibe-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> Two months later, an attempt was made to decapitate the Serra state located at <a href="/wiki/Mission_San_Gabriel_Arc%C3%A1ngel" title="Mission San Gabriel Arcángel">Mission San Gabriel Arcángel</a> with a <a href="/wiki/Reciprocating_saw" title="Reciprocating saw">reciprocating saw</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-failedbeheading_107-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-failedbeheading-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> However, the perpetrator instead settled for covering parts of the front area in red paint after the decapitation effort failed.<sup id="cite_ref-failedbeheading_107-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-failedbeheading-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>A bronze statue of Serra standing over an outline of the State of California previously stood in the <a href="/wiki/California_State_Capitol" title="California State Capitol">California State Capitol</a>'s Capitol Park. It faced a statue of <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Starr_King" title="Thomas Starr King">Thomas Starr King</a>, previously located in the <a href="/wiki/National_Statuary_Hall_Collection" title="National Statuary Hall Collection">National Statuary Hall Collection</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> It was later toppled by protestors on July 4, 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>A statue of Serra is located in the courtyard of <a href="/wiki/Mission_San_Francisco_de_As%C3%ADs" title="Mission San Francisco de Asís">Mission Dolores</a>, San Francisco's oldest remaining building.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li>
<li>A life-size bronze statue of Serra, which was removed in June 2020, overlooked the entrance to Mission Plaza in San Luis Obispo, near the façade of Old Mission San Luis Obispo.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Statues or other monuments to Father Serra are found on the grounds of several other mission churches, including those in San Diego and Santa Clara.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> A <a href="/wiki/Statue_of_Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra_(Carmel-by-the-Sea,_California)" title="Statue of Junípero Serra (Carmel-by-the-Sea, California)">statue on Serra Avenue</a> was removed by the city of Carmel on June 24, 2020 for safekeeping after some other statues in California were removed by protestors.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>A statue of Junipero Serra near the <a href="/wiki/San_Fernando_Mission" class="mw-redirect" title="San Fernando Mission">San Fernando Mission</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Mission_Hills,_Los_Angeles" title="Mission Hills, Los Angeles">Mission Hills</a> district of Los Angeles, California, was vandalized on August 17, 2017, as part of a larger movement to tear down monuments deemed offensive by activists.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_popular_culture">In popular culture</span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Fiction">Fiction</span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Robert_A._Heinlein" title="Robert A. Heinlein">Robert A. Heinlein</a> featured Serra in his serialized short fiction <a href="/wiki/Lost_Legacy" title="Lost Legacy">Lost Legacy</a> first published in November 1941 in <a href="/wiki/Super_Science_Stories" title="Super Science Stories">Super Science Stories</a> edited by <a href="/wiki/Frederick_Pohl" class="mw-redirect" title="Frederick Pohl">Frederick Pohl</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup>  The story is about a cache of ancient knowledge fictitiously discovered by Serra on Mt. Shasta. The fiction also features as a character <a href="/wiki/Ambrose_Bierce" title="Ambrose Bierce">Ambrose Bierce</a>, the famous writer who disappeared and was declared dead in 1913 at age 72.
</p>

<li id="cite_note-9"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-9">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20150927144004/http://www.ncregister.com/daily-news/pope-to-canonize-evangelizer-of-the-west-during-u.s.-trip/">"Pope to Canonize 'Evangelizer of the West' During U.S. Trip"</a>. <i>National Catholic Register</i>. Academy of American Franciscan History, 1959, vol. 1, pp. 86–87. Geiger discussed Serra's wound with three medical doctors—one of them Mexican—to fact-check this account from Serra and his first biographer, Francisco Palóu. Serra's wound may have been caused either by a mosquito bite or infestation by a "chigger," more precisely a <a href="/wiki/Tunga_penetrans" title="Tunga penetrans">chigoe flea</a>.</span>
